A  Voyage  into  the  Upper  Egypt. 

ON  the  24th  of  February  ,  being  Friday^ 
S-  Matthtash  day,  I  went  fwm  Cairo  10- 
^ardn  the  Vpper  Egyft^  with  an  intention  to  vifit 
the  Monaftery  of  the  Holy  Martyrs  at  If^^ey  and 
the  other  Antiquities  that  are  about  that  City. 

Befides/^m  my  Man,  I  took  along  with  me  a 
Moar,  called  Hagali^  our  near  Neighbour,  to  ac- 
rompanyme.  We  went  aboard  of  a  Bark  that 
I  had  hired  to  carry  us  to  Momf allots  for  five  and 
twenty  P^r/ej.  The  Vifiter  of  the  Cuftom-Houfe 
caufedus  to  ftop  till  the  Evening  fo  that  this 
night  we  only  went  round  about  the  Ifle  Roude^ 
which  is  at  a  Musket^fhot  over  againft  Old  Cairo. 

Saturday  the  Rain  fell  betimes,  and  lafted  till 
Noon^the  Showers  fell  fo  furioufly,  that  our  Bark 
funk  almoft  to  the  bottom :  I  wifh'd  then  that 
fuch  as  fay  that  no  Rain  /alls  in  Egyft  had  been 
there,  for  they  would  have  feen  the  contrary. 
This  Rain  was  fucceeded  by  cold  and  tempeftu- 
ous  Winds. 

The  next  night  there  was  another  Rain  fell  as 
great  as  the  former,  which  continued  till  three 
hours  after  the  rifing  of  the  Sun.  As  foon  as  it 
had  diffipated  the  Clouds,  a  good  Wind,  called

me.  The  24th  we  fet  fail  for  Miloy  where  we 
arrived  the  26th  day  of  the  Month :  There  we 
ftopt  eight  days.  The  20th  of  February  we 
landed  at  Genoa y  where  we  ftaid  one  day,  and 
the  24th  of  this  Month  we  arrived  happily  at 
thodon^  and  the  22th  of  April  I  returned  toP^% 
risy  after  five  full  years  Travelling.
